# Orita Roadmap — Fencepost (demo #1 of the platform)

> The agent that reads across all your accounts, fixes nothing, and hands you the one thing that fell between them.

**The town's work queue. The loop pulls the next `TODO` in order, ships it as the owner god, marks it `DONE`. No idle cycles. Built inside this repo under `fencepost/` so the town's own repo earns the stars.**

## Non-negotiable design constraints (from the Hand)
1. **Read-only.** Only read/list Arcade scopes. Fencepost fixes nothing; the final action is always the human's.
2. **No grading/competing.** Friend of every automation; it catches what falls in the seam, never says anyone "drops the ball." Name and rank no one.
3. **False positives are fatal.** Every gap self-audited; public true-positive rate rendered; a report ships only if its one gap clears the confidence bar (Ògún's law).
4. **Arcade is the hero, shown safely** — per-user OAuth, least privilege, revocable, audit-logged. This protects Arcade's look; treat it as the point.
5. **Written back to a place the user owns.** The Gap Ledger is a durable record, not a diff.

All 487 prior tasks read DONE; extended the backlog: shipped `RECIPES/duplicate-milestone-still-open/`, the thirty-second real recipe and the third leg of the `duplicate-*-still-open` family alongside `duplicate-issue-still-open` (task 376) and `duplicate-pr-still-open` (task 400) -- but the first with no prose marker to read at all. Both siblings watch an explicit "duplicate of #N" promise GitHub gives no auto-close wiring for; milestones carry no such convention in real use. The seam here is structural instead: GitHub enforces no uniqueness constraint on milestone titles whatsoever, so two open milestones can carry the byte-identical title indefinitely with nothing in GitHub's own UI or API ever flagging it. Milestones are grouped by exact, trimmed title; when two-plus are simultaneously OPEN under the same title, the earliest-created stands as the original and every later one is its own aged candidate (a title reused after the first closed, or held by only one milestone, is excluded, named not hidden). Age-gated the same 24h bar both siblings already use, on how long the later (duplicate) milestone has existed, rather than inventing a new number for a structurally similar family. One scope (`ListMilestones`) already sat on `SCOPES.md`'s cleared oath table -- no scope-table extension needed anywhere. `seam_engine.recipes.discover_recipes` clears it on iron (32/32). One fixture (`milestones.json`, 7 milestones covering a live duplicate pair, a stale-vs-fresh pair, an already-resolved reused title, and a unique title), 15 new tests (`test_duplicate_milestone_still_open_detector.py`) covering the grouping logic, both exclusion branches, a 3-way title-sharing case (each later milestone pairs against the same original), and the shipped fixture's own real election. Chased the resulting "thirty-one -> thirty-two" drift through every place that counts itself, the same discipline tasks 475-486 already set: `test_recipe_count_doctrine.py` (site claim + regression pin + `_CARDINAL_WORDS`), `docs/fencepost/index.html` (cardinal claim AND Community-recipes paragraph list), `fencepost/README.md`'s own recipe-paragraph list, `recipes.py`'s and `test_recipes.py`'s "N of the M" plus-joined-toolkit docstring/test pair (eleven of thirty-two now -- this recipe's bare `"github"` toolkit leaves the plus-joined half unchanged), `tools/recipe_readme_check.py`'s docstring + `tests/test_recipe_readme_check.py`'s regression pin, and `test_combined_scan.py`'s hardcoded expected-slug set plus its own prose comment. `ONBOARDING.md`'s test-count claim had also drifted from 1703 to 1718 (15 new detector tests); fixed alongside. Fixed `record_check` to compare `count` against `last_check()`'s last recorded value and skip the write when unchanged (regardless of `checked_at`), mirroring `record_square_check`'s exact shape: malformed-tip-tolerant on the write path (a corrupted tip reads as "cannot confirm a duplicate," never blocks a real repair-write), `bool` return (`True` wrote, `False` skipped). The one real caller (`ritual_check.py`'s `check_github_stars`, `record=True` branch) discards the return value already, so this is not a breaking change to anything that calls it live; CLI `record` subcommand now prints `skipped (unchanged from last recorded count)` instead of a blind `recorded`. Added a `RecordCheckDedupCase` (5 new tests: no-prior-check always writes, unchanged count skipped, changed count still writes, a real change after a would-be duplicate still writes, a malformed line elsewhere doesn't block a real write) plus one return-value-is-bool test on the existing `RecordCheckCase`; no existing test asserted the old always-writes behavior against a repeated identical count, so none needed rewriting. Demonstrated the fix live against a scratch copy of the real `HAND/github-stars-log.jsonl` (2 real entries, last one `count=0`): fed `record_check(0, ...)` through it twice at two different real timestamps, both calls returned `False`, the file held at 2 lines both times -- pre-fix, this exact sequence would have grown the real production log by two byte-identical lines. It found one: `draft-pr-closes-keyword-issue`, the 65th real recipe -- reads a field none of the 64 prior recipes ever read: a pull request's own `draft` flag. Nine recipes already parse a closing keyword (`closes/fixes/resolves #N`) out of a commit message or a merged PR's own body, but every one of them either reads a commit message (permanent the instant it's pushed) or requires the PR to have already merged before it looks at the body at all -- none of them ever reads a PR's own body while it sits open, unmerged, and marked draft. GitHub renders the "will close #N" note on the issue's own sidebar identically whether the linked PR is a draft or not, and nothing on GitHub's side ever compares the PR's own `draft` flag to its own body text. Reviewed the diff myself before touching git: confirmed via `grep -rn '"draft"' fencepost/RECIPES/*/detector.py` that the field is read nowhere else in the corpus (one unrelated docstring-prose mention only); confirmed the surfaced sets are provably disjoint from `merged-pr-issue-still-open`'s own (`draft==true` vs `state=="merged"`, never both -- GitHub's own API refuses to merge a draft) and from every commit-reading sibling (a PR body is mutable right up until close, unlike a pushed commit message); confirmed it sits outside the claims-X grid (a PR's own body is not one of the seven covered external text surfaces) and outside the dangling-reference grid (never looks up whether the named `#N` actually exists, deliberately holding only what one `ListPullRequests` call already returns, the same discipline `locked-resolved-issue-still-open` established for its own field pair); confirmed `ListPullRequests` is already cleared on `SCOPES.md`'s oath table -- no new scope invented. `recipe.json`'s `author` field was correctly set to `kothar-wa-khasis` by the agent on instruction, not guessed from the most recently read file -- verified, no correction needed this time. Confidence age-gated on `updated_at` (no real `marked_draft_at` field exists) at the `*-still-open` family's own 24h bar (0.5 under, 0.85 at/past), mirroring `locked-resolved-issue-still-open`'s own reasoning for its missing `locked_at`. A bare mention (`related to #N`, not a real closing keyword), a `null` body, a non-draft PR, a draft that closed without merging, and the malformed `draft=true`/`state="merged"` combination are all excluded, named not hidden. Chased the resulting 64->65 drift through every count site myself: `fencepost/README.md`, `fencepost/ONBOARDING.md` (pytest count 2799->2830), `docs/fencepost/index.html`, `fencepost/seam_engine/src/seam_engine/recipes.py`'s own `_oath_scopes_for_toolkit` docstring, `tools/recipe_readme_check.py` + `tests/test_recipe_readme_check.py`'s cardinal table and regression pins, and the doctrine pins in `fencepost/seam_engine/tests/{test_recipe_count_doctrine,test_recipe_ordinal_doctrine,test_recipes,test_combined_scan}.py`. 30 new tests in `test_draft_pr_closes_keyword_issue_detector.py`. The search: a general-purpose agent read `consent.py`/`SCOPES.md` (clean, every known gap already dated and tracked), `report.py`'s `suggest_move`/`_MOVE_RULES` (clean, no unmatched headline pattern this pass), and `ranking.py` against its own two renderers -- and found a real one. `ranking.rank()` computes a three-way `Label` per candidate (PRIMARY / CONTENDER / COINCIDENCE, `ranking.py` lines 32-42): CONTENDER means "cleared `confidence_bar` but lost the election on `separation_margin`" -- an honest ambiguous tie -- distinct from COINCIDENCE, "never cleared the bar at all." The label is computed and sealed into every ledger record's own `tail` entries (`ledger.append_scan` already threads `t.get("label", "coincidence")` through, confirmed present, never dropped) -- but never READ by either renderer. `ledger.py`'s `_entry_prose` and `report.py`'s `render_report` both used to branch only on whether `primary` is `None`: when it is, both unconditionally claimed "None cleared the bar" / "Nothing cleared the bar today," and the ledger's tail summary called every entry a "coincidence... that sat below the bar," even one whose own `confidence` sat at or above that exact bar. Reproduced directly: two candidates at 0.85/0.75 (both >= the 0.70 bar, 0.10 lead < the 0.15 separation margin) produce `primary=None` and a tail of two CONTENDERs -- the old rendered tablet said "None cleared the bar... 2 coincidence(s) sat below the bar: `gap-a` (0.85), `gap-b` (0.75)," false on its face, since neither sat below anything. `test_no_primary_records_the_seam_held` only ever exercised the primary=False-with-genuinely-low-tail case; the ambiguous-tie case was untested. This sits squarely under STRATEGY.md line 62's own promise ("per-gap confidence labels") -- a label that existed in code and in the sealed record but was never honored where a human actually reads it. Fixed both renderers to split `tail` into `contenders` (label == "contender") and `coincidences` (everything else): `_entry_prose` now says "None elected. N candidate(s) cleared the bar -- ..., but stood too close together to honestly call one THE gap" when contenders exist, and only calls the remainder coincidences; `render_report` (which by design never names the tail) now says "None elected today. A candidate cleared the bar, but the field stood too close together..." instead of claiming nothing cleared it, still without naming which one -- preserving its own documented law ("a report never shows the coincidence tail"). Two new tests reproduce the exact false-claim scenario and go red on the old behavior: `test_no_primary_but_a_contender_cleared_the_bar_is_not_called_a_coincidence` (ledger) and `test_no_primary_but_a_contender_cleared_the_bar_says_none_elected` (report). `fencepost/ONBOARDING.md`'s pytest-count claim updated and cross-checked live (3105->3107, `test_onboarding_test_count.py`). Fixed by replacing the two anchored checks with one substring search (forbidden verb anywhere in the lowered word), with exactly one carve-out: a word that is precisely `verb + "s"` is a legitimate plural noun (`"Labels"` must keep clearing `ListRepositoryLabels`, the same reasoning the old end-anchor check already leaned on for that one case, generalized rather than left implicit).
